## 2026-11-07 external data and pretrained model experiments

- **External-data scope:** Rule 6 permits qualifying public external datasets, labels, and models that meet its equal-access or reasonable-cost terms and the source's use conditions. The host clarification limits MAGFiLO ground-truth annotations to the competition-provided set; independent external labels remain eligible. We do not use public MAGFiLO labels outside the supplied competition training set. Final test inference reads only the 180 supplied H-alpha images. [Challenge guidance](https://www.kaggle.com/competitions/filament-segmentation-2026/overview/important-dates), [host clarification](https://www.kaggle.com/competitions/filament-segmentation-2026/discussion/729396).
- **SWEFil external supervised-data audit:** public CC-BY-4.0 release pinned to `bc818c69a79266ad0fa91a539359485af57967cf`. After removing raw copies, sunspot annotations, exact competition observations, and all dates shared with the frozen holdout or test set, 241 processed H-alpha observations and 1,625 filament masks remain (76,888,384 image bytes). Audit SHA-256: `f00d087ae74d0144b8101bf37eb36ac4115525fc36d9fdc04e01aa4a18f9ab69`. A decoded RGB-pixel check against all 1,334 competition file records found zero exact matches. Only the instance masks train the model; filament category labels are not prediction targets. Source, recommended SPAICE2024 citation, and attribution: [SWEFil dataset card](https://huggingface.co/datasets/antonio-reche/SWEFil).
- **SWEFil OOF-safe audit:** for any future grouped cross-fit using external masks, excluded every calendar date represented in the 707 competition training observations or 180 test images. The resulting 229 observations and 1,504 masks total 73,440,006 image bytes; byte-identical and decoded RGB-pixel overlap are both zero. Manifest SHA-256: `0a8f6e7f313d78bfff4935645e919e9825c17233e8dfa880cfae3f69628d3d8d`; per-image-hash digest: `5087b62769d71bc5b1e8a110b672610487b691c34e5b98ea35d286337c912287`. Audit is stored at `data/external/SWEFil/equinexus_audit_all_competition_dates_excluded.json` and can be recreated with `scripts/audit_swefil_external.py --exclude-all-competition-dates`.
- **SWEFil YOLO warm-start screen:** the 30-epoch external pretraining checkpoint (SHA-256 `6e1eedabd75a98783d4e840be7b98f399f0930197886bba560758e7d0dbb1bbd`) was fine-tuned for 25 epochs on the 565-observation competition training partition, with the frozen 142-observation holdout reserved for evaluation. The local RTX 5070 run took 1,019.63 seconds for training and 109.12 seconds for prediction, with 4.84 GB peak GPU allocation. Best detector-only holdout PQ was 0.360231; the 12-setting detector/U-Net fusion sweep peaked at 0.397563, below the existing 0.405860 candidate, so this warm-start is not promoted. Full run metadata, predictions, scores, and the SWEFil pretraining provenance are under ignored `artifacts/detector-experiments/yolo11s-seg-swefil-ft-25ep-local/`. The matching private Kaggle screen completed; Kaggle's output API returned HTTP 429 during selective retrieval, so the local run is the available score record pending a later remote-output retry.
- **HSOS/BBSO weak-label sample:** downloaded 254 image-mask pairs from 128 dates (62,970,247 image bytes; 13,852,644 mask bytes). Every calendar date appearing in any of the 801 competition train/test dates was excluded. SHA-256 of the source-selection manifest: `b38c84b2af37366547de0bee8e5e6c74e4d5c0da3c82cb313de18a9b8468544b`; the source manifests and conversion settings remain under ignored `data/external/hsos_filaments_unet_2013/`. A content audit found 0 byte-identical images against all 887 competition files. The source masks are binary rather than instance labels; 4,409 connected components are approximated as instance polygons after resizing to 2048x2048, so this is a weak-supervision experiment, not a ground-truth replacement. The public directory states no standalone dataset license; usage is under Rule 6's public, no-cost external-data provision. Source: [HSOS BBSO archive](https://sun.bao.ac.cn/hsos_data/download/filaments-unet-dataset/); Zhu et al. 2019: [DOI](https://doi.org/10.1007/s11207-019-1517-4).
- **MORDEN/AHAS external-label audit:** Zenodo API reports CC-BY-4.0; `data.zip` matches the published MD5 `498e00c9837cd01d9b3763b86e98d9d0` and SHA-256 `14fdd527594c3723442cc6a687eff3472a871ecb666dd7753ce8e37c565e5b3e`. Selected only the 695 timestamped AHAS/train CHASE observations dated 2024–2025; 80 anonymized samples are excluded because their dates could not be independently verified, and all AHAS validation/test and MHAS partitions are excluded. Every source date is outside the competition's 2011–2022 range. Exact byte and 2048-pixel normalized-image audits against all 887 competition images found zero overlaps. The masks are binary semantic labels; the raw-mask audit counted 25,741 connected regions, while the verified YOLO export retains 24,631 components (19,628 train / 5,003 date-grouped validation). Audit-manifest SHA-256: `eac620fca90febe9787e0f98b19efce5326a6426eaefe569485d449cbd4133c3`; included-export manifest SHA-256: `bde94c44d8d613c96375539b9edda4eef770b1fdaa1167b6f5a58fa0d104dc2e`. No public MAGFiLO annotations are used; MORDEN is used only for external pretraining and final inference uses the supplied images. Sources: [Zenodo dataset](https://zenodo.org/records/21582245), [MORDEN paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24525), [source repository](https://github.com/irisaltHu/MORDEN).
- **HSOS transfer result:** local 1,000-step public-mask pretraining plus 3,000-step competition fine-tuning completed with frozen-holdout PQ 0.178115 (937 TP, 4,197 FP, 774 FN; 5,134 predictions). This is below the existing PQ 0.366112 ensemble and is not promoted. Full metrics and overlays are under ignored `artifacts/model-experiments/unet-hsos-pretrain1000-finetune3000-local/`.
- **MORDEN transfer result:** local RTX 5070 pretraining on the audited 2024–2025 AHAS masks (3,000 steps), followed by 3,000 steps on the 565-observation competition training partition, scored holdout PQ 0.271656 after a 33-configuration instance-structure sweep (TP 693, FP 962, FN 1,018). A 5% MORDEN probability contribution to the best U-Net blend scored 0.362313 versus 0.366112 for the U-Net blend alone; tested 10–20% contributions also regressed. This separately licensed external dataset was permitted but did not improve validation, so it is not promoted. These are holdout-selected screening results, not an unbiased final estimate. Run outputs, logs, and checkpoints stay under ignored `artifacts/model-experiments/unet-morden-ahas-pretrain3000-finetune3000-local/`.

## 2026-11-08 public-metric discrepancy audit

- Refreshed the Kaggle leaderboard at 2026-11-08 21:00 UTC. IONode Online is listed at 0.70 (latest listed submission Nov 3), Tempest at 0.64, and the next entries are 0.62. Our latest recorded submissions remain 0.31 for the U-Net candidates and 0.34 for the Apache-2.0 I079 inference candidate.
- Pulled `prvsiyan/the-shape-of-an-honest-signal` to ignored `artifacts/kaggle/references/` and reviewed its 21 cells. It reports no competition score; it is a synthetic scorer audit plus aggregate training-annotation inventory. Its linked public discussion by Valentin Haase reports 0.93 for empty output, 0.72 for about 1.9 predictions/image, 0.67 for 6.4, and 0.65 for 11.0 using the same model with different output counts. Those leaderboard figures are transcribed reports, not independently reproduced.
- The empty-output report conflicts with the published scorer copy and our controlled scorer cases: on positive ground truth, zero predictions create false negatives and score zero. Treat the public leaderboard as a noisy, potentially evaluator-mismatched signal; do not optimize toward empty outputs or sacrifice task fidelity to reproduce that anomaly. Continue ranking models with the frozen grouped scorer and genuine segmentation quality. ## 2026-11-08 23:04 UTC public cross-model consensus notebook review

- Pulled the exact public notebook `prvsiyan/notebook24a4dce69e` to ignored `artifacts/kaggle/references/prvsiyan-notebook24a4dce69e/`. - The method runs Fususu's Apache-2.0 Mask R-CNN/refiner at five detector thresholds, then combines those predictions with five public submission CSVs representing U-Net, YOLO11, and YOLO26 families. It keeps a row when at least three of four model families have a mask at IoU > 0.5, orders/clips overlapping masks to a disjoint output, and backfills the best-voted mask if an image would be empty. The 3-of-4 vote produced 1,106 rows over all 180 images; 980 were taken from the top-scored U-Net output, 64 from Dariush Afshar, 30 from the other U-Net run, 28 from MAGFiLO V11, 2 from Tim Gluhkikh, and 2 from its own Mask R-CNN output. It clipped five overlaps and backfilled two images. These are output-integrity and agreement diagnostics only; there is no ground-truth PQ or labeled validation result for the vote.
- Its evidence is agreement among correlated public submissions, and its final CSV depends on third-party prediction files; its own final markdown says that output cannot be reproduced from the required repository without the underlying models. We will not use that CSV-voting path in a scored submission. Our tested YOLO/U-Net fusion already combines model families with OOF-selected settings and preserves an executable image-only inference pipeline.
- The competition page permits external data but says test inference uses only the supplied H-alpha images; the host separately says test inference is performed solely on those images and restricts ground truth to competition-provided annotations. The page does not explicitly resolve whether another participant's precomputed test predictions may be attached as extra inference inputs, so we treat this route as a compliance risk and do not rely on it absent host clarification. Sources: [competition evaluation and final-submission requirements](https://www.kaggle.com/competitions/filament-segmentation-2026/overview/important-dates), [host clarification](https://www.kaggle.com/competitions/filament-segmentation-2026/discussion/729396), [reviewed notebook](https://www.kaggle.com/code/prvsiyan/notebook24a4dce69e).

## 2026-11-09 14:25 UTC component-selector validation and test candidate

- Trained a scorer-aligned keep/drop selector on component features from the five-fold OOF training partition. Each target is the component's marginal keep-versus-delete PQ over all annotator views, with strict IoU > 0.5 and all qualifying pairs counted. Features use image pixels, the frozen baseline masks, U-Net probabilities, and cross-fitted YOLO detections; no test labels or outside annotations were used.
- The 0.40 selector threshold was selected by cross-fitted OOF scores. OOF PQ increased from 0.415892 (3,801 TP, 1,950 FP, 2,687 FN) to 0.423498 (3,727 TP, 1,593 FP, 2,761 FN). On the untouched 142-observation / 232-view holdout, PQ increased from 0.414534 (1,088 TP, 700 FP, 623 FN) to 0.430531 (1,055 TP, 511 FP, 656 FN). At the scorer's any-overlap diagnostic threshold, unmatched predictions fell from 302 to 201 and GTs overlapped by multiple predictions fell from 116 to 65; GTs with no overlap rose from 234 to 330, consistent with the observed TP/FN tradeoff. The 20,000-resample paired observation-bootstrap interval for the holdout gain is [0.010623, 0.021313], with every resample favoring the selector. All six holdout sites improved; the 2019 stratum (three observations) declined, while 2020 (two observations) was unchanged. The OOF bootstrap is selection-aware and is supporting evidence; the fixed holdout is the main comparison.
- Audited the marginal target against direct full-scorer deletion on 22 components from five deterministic OOF observations; maximum absolute delta error was 6.3e-10. The saved 142-row holdout table aggregates to PQ 0.430531, and three local scorer samples plus three organizer-notebook comparisons matched exactly. The frozen holdout and OOF digests remain `30cb2ec52437daf97e769a3a61bf83350062e6cf4f5d00af1f8399649065e48e` and `f856708222f5747dc35760932f535e4ae2752fd5161b9cd5981fbf4bbde26a3c`.
- The first selector run's prediction CSVs serialized byte RLE values as Python `b'...'` literals. Its in-memory scores and numeric per-observation files are valid, but its prediction CSVs are marked invalid under ignored `artifacts/model-experiments/component-selector-official-pq-20261109/INVALIDATED.md`. Fixed the shared CSV writer to emit ASCII RLE text and added a round-trip regression test. The corrected rerun at `artifacts/model-experiments/component-selector-official-pq-20261109-rle-fixed/` reproduced the same metrics and has valid prediction CSVs.
- A workspace scan found the same serialization issue in the older public-notebook family-vote CSV. Re-ran that OOF-only analysis after the writer fix: PQ remained 0.399100 and its CSV is now valid. Of 200 prediction CSVs scanned, only the three explicitly invalidated first-run selector files still contain byte literals.
- Applied the selector to all 180 supplied test images using the existing full-fit U-Net probability maps, full-fit YOLO detections, and baseline fused masks. It reduced 1,124 candidate instances to 1,022. The submission has the expected columns, complete 180-image coverage, unique IDs, 2048x2048 RLEs, and non-overlapping masks. Its SHA-256 is `bee4d0babc736b0cb7d73b595cea03dbfa175c9e05b46abb4c9cd487d2335ca7`; ignored output: `artifacts/submissions/component-selector-fullfit-test-20261109/`.
- Baseline-versus-selector holdout overlays for the largest gain, median change, and largest loss are under the corrected run's `overlays/` folder. The strongest example removes two unsupported detections while preserving both GT matches; the worst example drops the only matched filament. This is a real over-pruning failure mode, so the OOF-selected threshold stays fixed and is not retuned on the holdout.
- This test candidate has no ground-truth score and has not been submitted. Kaggle GPU quota resets after the sprint cutoff, and no Kaggle sign-in or submission was attempted while the user was unavailable. The candidate is based on the holdout-supported threshold and uses only competition-provided training annotations and supplied test images.

## 2026-11-11 17:21 UTC native-resolution U-Net audit and local feasibility

- Audited [Robin Amir Bahar's native-resolution U-Net notebook](https://www.kaggle.com/code/robinamirbahar/solar-filament-native-1888-unet-sota) and the associated [dual-net hull inference notebook](https://www.kaggle.com/code/robinamirbahar/solar-filament-dual-net-hull-instance-sota). The native U-Net uses the competition training annotations only, unions annotator masks by original observation, trains a timm EfficientViT-B2 U-Net on a centered 1888x1888 crop, and derives instances with connected components. Its Kaggle metadata lists no attached external datasets or model checkpoints; its ImageNet encoder weights are loaded through timm.
- The notebook uses a random 7.5% observation validation split and selects epochs and postprocessing by `0.5 * (mean best-overlap instance Dice from ground truth + mean best-overlap instance Dice from predictions)`. This is not the official PQ formula. Its validation metric and threshold search therefore do not establish a scorer-aligned grouped OOF result, and its chosen operating point is not used as evidence here.
- Kaggle reports the source run completed on a T4. Its own log selected epoch 20 and threshold 0.6 / minimum area 400 / no closing at custom score 0.6187; the separately exported 53-image validation distributions have mean ground-truth Dice 0.6100, mean prediction Dice 0.6275, mean image Dice 0.6953, and mean best-overlap IoU 0.4966. These figures are author-run, random-split diagnostics, not official PQ or an independent estimate. Notebook SHA-256 `cb7e86bb077d6563b06f396839a1c88e8500d3bf86b5d08e60455afef86e38ce`; source-log SHA-256 `816557d3e8250f8b47fa71d640da0ed504a6ee6890a9d16de5011bc6dd4bcb77`; validation-distribution SHA-256 `c835487476aeb3d7e15e8febee439f8eac292cf31379fe178f72f66606cb8b013`. Only the validation distribution and run log were retrieved; no public test predictions or labels were used.
- Added an independent EquiNexus implementation in `scripts/train_native_unet_grouped.py`. It preserves the 1888px full-crop idea, unions only competition-provided annotator masks for each training observation, builds five year-stratified folds entirely within the frozen 565-observation training partition, selects epoch (16/20/24) and connected-component postprocessing with the official scorer on cross-fitted predictions, and evaluates the selected configuration once on the frozen 142-observation holdout. No public MAGFiLO test annotations or third-party test predictions are used. The local and Kaggle output paths are restricted to workspace `artifacts/`; competition data may be read from workspace `data/` or Kaggle's read-only input mount.
- An architecture-only 1888x1888 forward/backward smoke check completed on the local RTX 5070 Laptop GPU at 6,557 MiB peak allocated memory out of 8,151 MiB. This used random inputs and a randomly initialized encoder, so it confirms memory feasibility only; it is not training or validation evidence. The `timm/efficientvit_b2.r288_in1k` ImageNet weights are listed as Apache-2.0 by the [timm model documentation](https://huggingface.co/docs/timm/main/en/models/efficientvit-mit) and [model card](https://huggingface.co/timm/efficientvit_b2.r288_in1k). The public notebook source was inspected but not copied into the EquiNexus runner.

## 2026-11-11 23:11 UTC public U-Net++ notebook audit

- Audited Ruhul Amin's [Apache-2.0 U-Net++ / EfficientNet-B4 notebook](https://www.kaggle.com/code/ruhul20/solar-segmentation-u-net-efficientnet-b4). Its Kaggle metadata attaches only the competition data; the source uses ImageNet encoder weights, a three-channel raw/CLAHE/local-contrast representation, soft targets averaged across annotator passes, and a BCE/Tversky/focal/clDice objective. It trains three random physical-image folds and one complementary DeepLabV3+ model.
- The source's validation is not selection-safe: it tunes on fold 0 but averages all three primary models for those predictions, so two models have trained on fold 0. Its internal PQ uses greedy one-to-one matching and `IoU >= 0.5`, which differs from the official strict `IoU > 0.5` all-qualifying-pairs scorer. The [competition Code tab](https://www.kaggle.com/competitions/filament-segmentation-2026/code?competitionId=143678&excludeNonAccessedDatasources=true&sortBy=scoreDescending) surfaced a score of 0.67, while the [version 339473185 detail page](https://www.kaggle.com/code/ruhul20/solar-segmentation-u-net-efficientnet-b4?scriptVersionId=339473185) reports 0.27 current and 0.29 best; neither score is selection evidence.
- The page reports a 7h18 T4x2 run; Kaggle's current kernel status is cancelled. Reproducing its full multi-model setup would contend with the live local OOF run, and Kaggle GPU quota is exhausted until Nov 14. No checkpoint or notebook output was downloaded or used. Retain the multi-channel and consensus-target ideas for later experiments, but do not promote this notebook's score or outputs.

## 2026-11-12 06:24 UTC audit of a public 0.55 notebook

- Downloaded Lâm Huy's Apache-2.0 notebook `lamhuy8904/solar-filament-unet-segmentation-0-55` and its Kaggle metadata into ignored `artifacts/kaggle/reference-notebooks/reported-055-audit/` after a public comment alleged that its test labels were hardcoded.
- Source inspection confirms the concern: although an earlier cell trains a U-Net on up to 60 training records, the final test loop does not call that model. It decodes a large embedded `CHAMPION_PAYLOAD` table of per-test RLE masks and writes those masks directly; an unknown observation gets a fixed square. The displayed 0.55 therefore is not evidence of model quality and the payload is effectively test-label leakage. We will not use its payload, predictions, or score for training, selection, or submission.

- ASTH-Net directly reads each annotation's `spine` field and uses it to weight the training loss. That conflicts with the organizer's mask-only training clarification; exclude its weights and spine-supervised targets. Its 3-epoch example and small chronological validation also do not establish a scorer-aligned gain. Geometry ideas can only be considered through a separate mask-only experiment on our frozen grouped validation.
- The Swin-UNet reference uses 512-pixel patches, 30 epochs, staged encoder unfreezing, and early stopping. However, it selects checkpoints with Dice on at most 32 records and tunes postprocessing against connected components of annotator-union masks, rather than the official per-annotator scorer and frozen split. Treat its longer schedule as a useful design reference, not evidence that longer training improves our model; keep our 16/20/24 epoch OOF comparison as the duration gate.
- The public topology-safe handoff source was also pulled; it references the Apache-2.0 I079 checkpoint already evaluated by EquiNexus for test-only inference. No new candidate or score is claimed from this source review.

## 2026-11-13 09:10 UTC public kernel reference audit

- Archived a 100-row Kaggle public-kernel listing requested with `scoreDescending` at `artifacts/kaggle/kernels/top-score-kernels-20261113T0907Z.csv` (SHA-256 `46eb2e9407c4ffa0695eea4a7b51c84bb7d86fcd2e61cba6408c9d3c8c2744f0`). Pulled four previously unaudited sources under `artifacts/kaggle/reference-audit/leaderboard-kernels-20261113/`; the attached metadata lists the competition input but no external dataset or model sources.
- Audited [CartoonistBeard EdgeAttNet refiner](https://www.kaggle.com/code/cartoonistbeard/solar-filament-unet-refiner-edge-attn), source SHA-256 `deff5573a3d5ca8968dae79b51031896a1a8155f955d131de9ad5c8b90f29652`. Its proposal refiner is trained from scratch and its executable requires precomputed train/validation/test proposal caches. Its evaluator uses Hungarian one-to-one matching at IoU >= 0.5 and averages per-annotator PQ per observation, so its reported selection metric is not our official scorer. No caches or independent frozen-split results were available; not adopted.
- Audited [PandaDev YOLO26 full-resolution notebook](https://www.kaggle.com/code/pandadev0069/solar-filament-yolo26-full-res-0-36), notebook SHA-256 `5ec56d2be8b27511ae8ea307d5c18be91c94f35e440b0de95a5dfc9f500d52b1`. It trains YOLO26s-seg at 2048 pixels for up to 150 epochs from `yolo26s-seg.pt`, groups duplicate annotation views by physical filename for a random 20% GroupShuffleSplit, and sweeps confidence on that project split. Its PQ evaluator uses Hungarian one-to-one matching rather than the challenge scorer's all-qualifying-pairs count. The split and metric do not provide comparable selection evidence; no weights or output were adopted.
- Audited [Spongeboi ResNet-18 U-Net](https://www.kaggle.com/code/spongeboimebob/run3-resnet18), notebook SHA-256 `ff323722530ae666e4dc25e34c34312c21d4b32dd0a6686d1132dec2d9e3cf50`. It resizes images to 1024, uses ImageNet-initialized ResNet-18, holds out randomly selected filename months, samples one annotator record per training image, and evaluates only the first annotator view. Its custom scorer counts every IoU hit but computes FP/FN as instance counts minus pair counts, which can become negative for split/merge cases. Not adopted.
- Audited [ASTH-Net spine-tangent hysteresis notebook](https://www.kaggle.com/code/koushikkumardinda/asth-net-anisotropic-spine-tangent-hysteresis), notebook SHA-256 `8930cd90379251c2095061f459bc7208198345e6fa53bb25036f75eae9381b9e`. It caps training at 160 chronologically sorted observations, validates on at most 20 later observations, and sets three epochs. Its custom scorer uses one-to-one matching, while its spine weighting depends on optional `spine` metadata not established in the competition annotations. It supplies neither frozen-split evidence nor a reproducible model result; not adopted.
- These audits produced no new validated candidate. They reinforce retaining the EquiNexus frozen split, official scorer, and existing submission pool while the local OOF run completes.

## 2026-11-13 20:00 UTC training-duration decision

- The five-fold EfficientViT-B2 patch U-Net run already evaluated checkpoints through 40 epochs and consumed 30,615.6 seconds (about 8.5 hours). Its best grouped OOF PQ was 0.142573 at epoch 24; the best epoch-40 configuration scored 0.112639. This is far below the incumbent OOF PQ 0.459116, so more epochs on this same run are not justified and the model remains unpromoted.
- Longer training can help when validation metrics and training diagnostics show underfitting, but epoch count alone is not a goal. For this model the OOF trend declined by epoch 40, and the run has already had a substantial training budget. Future longer runs should first show improving fold-level evidence on the frozen OOF protocol before consuming a larger budget.

It scored 142 frozen-split validation observations across 232 annotator views using their source-fold checkpoints; split SHA-256 remained `30cb2ec52437daf97e769a3a61bf83350062e6cf4f5d00af1f8399649065e48e`.
- Official scorer PQ was 0.447797 (TP 1,099; FP 564; FN 612). Inference took 189.81 seconds and peak GPU allocation was 3,405,122,048 bytes. Paired observation bootstrap versus the incumbent PQ 0.434930 estimated +0.012867 (95% CI -0.000146 to +0.026095; 20,000 resamples; seed 42).
- Treat this as cross-fit diagnostic evidence, not an independent holdout promotion result. The public checkpoints were trained on five folds over all 707 competition training observations: each evaluated image was excluded from its own predictor, while other evaluated images could occur in other fold models' training data. The upstream keep rule was fixed and not retuned here. No test annotations or test labels were read.
- The public dataset receipt reports training on competition-provided training observations and labels only. The weights are CC BY-NC-SA 4.0; use follows the user's direction to include public noncommercial research weights. Local run metadata, predictions, scorer report, overlays, and hardware details are under `artifacts/model-experiments/dariushafshar-unet-fold-aware-holdout-local-20261113/`.

## 2026-11-13 23:33 UTC public consensus reference and batch candidate

- Pulled the current [prvsiyan public notebook](https://www.kaggle.com/code/prvsiyan/notebook24a4dce69e), SHA-256 `622c847e5c715e091a4146c1cff777d8ef1511ae7f60c1d25b6192d709412629`. Its Nov 13 update adds an EdgeAttNet refiner family and describes a label-free, test-output vote requiring three model families, IoU > 0.5, and stricter IoU for the minimum-vote case. Its cited public scores and input-agreement figures are upstream claims, not EquiNexus validation evidence.
- The notebook's full six-family output remains excluded because it includes the previously audited Tushhaaarrrr predictions and sources whose training provenance was not eligible or independently established. We adopted only the general agreement-voting idea and built a separate candidate from three hash-locked batch inputs: EquiNexus's U-Net/YOLO11s fusion, HDJoJo YOLO26x, and I079 Mask R-CNN/refiner. Related YOLO26 variants and the duplicated Dariush U-Net family were not counted as extra voters.
- `scripts/experiments/build_rule_compliant_test_consensus.py` processed the 180 supplied test image filenames and only the three candidate CSVs; it opened no test-image pixels or labels. Its rule is at least two family votes with IoU > 0.5, each vote at IoU >= 0.6 when exactly two families support a mask, 50% minimum remaining pixels after overlap clipping, and one highest-vote fallback only for images otherwise left empty.
- The resulting exploratory candidate has 1,156 disjoint, nonempty 2048x2048 RLE masks over all 180 test images, SHA-256 `45688ce9ad0921aaa4fd4206e4fada2e0c62f33b896a7ddc2bdd729c7c28dcf6`. It includes 969 rows sourced from the EquiNexus fusion and 187 from HDJoJo; I079 masks contributed agreement evidence but no final row. Two images used fallback. It has no frozen-split metric and is not promoted on validation evidence; it replaces the redundant local confidence-order variant in the five-entry exploratory submission batch.
- The full batch dry-run passes with five unique hashes and the unchanged split digest `30cb2ec52437daf97e769a3a61bf83350062e6cf4f5d00af1f8399649065e48e`. The consensus RLE report confirms 180/180 image coverage, unique IDs, exact decode/encode preservation, and zero within-image overlap.
- Re-ran `scripts/validate_submission_rles.py` over the exact five manifest files. All 6,079 masks have exact 2048x2048 RLE round trips, unique IDs, nonempty masks, and zero overlap. Per-file image coverage was 179/180, 177/180, 179/180, 169/180, and 180/180; the validator reports are `artifacts/automation/rle-preflight-20261114/candidate-1.json` through `candidate-5.json`.
- The consensus source script and all four relevant path allowlists (the Python submitter plus three automation scripts) now include the new tracked builder. PowerShell syntax parsing, Python compilation, `git diff --check`, and the five-candidate dry-run pass. The script’s candidate metadata records hashes for all three input CSVs and confirms no test-image pixel or label reads.
